For the active checklist, see [Current status and to-do](TODO.md). Older entries record decisions, test evidence, and pending work as of their dates.

## Phrase takes and sparse fills in lesson Auto-sync

The stricter take comparison and the fill search are now connected to the browser worker. Normal lesson Auto-sync may choose a better supported take. Fill proposals open in the draft reviewer, where Use reviewed timing activates them. Real-button checks included R&B Fill 1, Chord Scale 6 and Easy Guitar Playalong. [Comparison and limitations](AUTO-SYNC-BASELINE.md#whole-phrase-take-selection-and-searches-with-no-initial-match)

## Online audio for an untimed send

Auto-sync first tries the recording URL. On a fetch failure it can read the same selected recording through a compatible sender and run its usual analysis. This supersedes the separate local-copy picker below. Local recordings still use the ordinary media loader. A live isolated Chrome test sent the original Phrygian Mode GPX and selected video URL with no timing, using test-profile site grants. The direct read failed, the extension supplied the recording in memory, 24 of 25 visits were selected and measure 23 appeared at 4:59.69. The actual linked video remained loaded.

## Linked video playback and local audio analysis

The cached Phrygian Mode test used a local MP4. The archived TrueFire media redirect answered a one-byte request from the localhost origin with an allow-origin header, and its CDN returned partial content without that header. The analyzer now offers a matching local file on link read failures, preserving the linked playback source and retrying the original Analyze or Auto-sync action. Reference timing labels alone do not establish that speech occurs. Verified in isolated Chrome on the live Phrygian Mode URL: retrying with its cached MP4 retained the remote video and reproduced the 4:59.69 suggestion for measure 23. The synthetic link test also rejected a shorter copy and resumed successfully with the matching file.

## Auditioning missing measures in an audio draft

The reviewer shows each proposed measure and repeat pass alongside its alternative recording times. Play with lead-in starts two seconds before a take and ends at its boundary. Use this take inserts it through the manual measure editor’s interval and undo handling. One-click Auto-sync users can reach the retained review through Edit sync and Edit timed measures. A saved timing profile stores the accepted visits. Exported draft work also retains the unchosen suggestions. Owner listening feedback is pending.

## Recovering only the music beside a break

A blind benchmark on 200 archived pages compared independent forward and backward recovery from current Auto-sync placements. The candidate-confirmed rule recovered The Phrygian Mode measure 23 without covering its breakdown, but also selected a passage 443.69 s late on Country Shuffle Electric Guitar Rhythm. This remains an offline experiment. [Method, attack counts and controls](AUTO-SYNC-BASELINE.md#partial-recovery-beside-a-recording-break)

## Filling missing phrases in lesson recordings

Compared seven variants over all 200 archived lesson pages. The shipped rule added 94 placements on the 197 comparable pages, with 91 within 250 ms at both ends. Existing endpoints stayed fixed and the new placements added zero overlap with labeled breaks. [Results, limits and replay commands](AUTO-SYNC-BASELINE.md#recovery-between-two-synced-measures)

## Blind Auto-sync benchmark across archived Lesson Bridge lessons

Auto-sync ran on all 200 identified lesson pages with the reference timing withheld. Across 197 duration-checked recordings, it placed 3,568 of 5,235 timed measure visits. Both endpoints were within 250 ms on 2,249 visits. See the [baseline, owner-checked examples and per-lesson results](AUTO-SYNC-BASELINE.md).

## Continuous checkbox in the Media Sync toolbar

The Continuous checkbox sits beneath Auto-sync and uses the browser's blue checkmark control. The owner reports that the converted Arirang timing tracked the performance closely, with slight drift on a few notes during within-measure tempo changes. The whole-performance conversion is specific to applied audio-generated timing. Drafts received with marked explanations retain those breaks.

## Filling the gaps of a continuous performance

The owner spot-checked the recovered Arirang opening and timing through the end. The resulting draft still omitted 32 visits and had ten gaps between adjacent mapped measures. A local Chrome test converted its 148 mapped visits to 236 standard points. All matched downbeats and interior anchors retained their recording times. The musical boundaries remained 1.32 and 359.28 seconds. All 1,694 expected practice events across the expanded 180 visits became available. The test added a point at beat two of a previously omitted visit, saved the standard profile, restored the original draft and switched back with the edit intact.

The conversion estimates the timing of the missing music. The longest previously unmapped run was about 6.91 seconds starting at 2:55.31. Owner listening through the interpolated runs and physical MIDI grading are pending.

## Continuous music in Media Sync

Added a checkbox beside Auto-sync for single performances. In a local Chrome run on Yenne Lee’s *Arirang Fantasy*, the ordinary scan selected 127 of 180 measure visits. Continuous music selected 148, with 23 additional candidates available to audition. Mapped time increased from 248.3 to 301.7 seconds. Measures 1, 2 and 3 started at 1.32, 4.75 and 7.94 seconds. The estimated last chord ran from 5:55.00 to 5:59.28. The draft carried 67 interior attack anchors across 40 visits. These are analyzer estimates. A reference timing file was unavailable for this performance.

With the box unchecked, the saved comparison of 24 lessons returned identical take candidates and choices, including all 458 previously selected visits. On Carry My Name, 35 of 40 placed visits were within 0.25 seconds of the reference at both ends in each mode. Chrome also exercised rerunning Auto-sync from an accepted audio draft. Automated regressions cover a held note, a silent gap, written tempo changes and review-only choices. Listening and physical MIDI verification on Arirang are pending.

## Continuous performance trial with rubato

A local browser run on a 6:15 solo guitar performance placed 127 of 180 repeat-expanded visits. The GPX imported as a single classical guitar track with low D (MIDI 38). The first two measures contain the same notes. All candidates retained for those two measures were later than the selected M3 at 7.94 s. Backward recovery skips rows that already have candidates. Interior recovery also expects the local duration to fit both neighbors to within 15%, which can be too restrictive for rubato. The resulting draft omitted 53 visits and had another 12 gaps between consecutive mapped measures.

A local experiment followed the full written order with flexible timing, using three different pitch and time resolutions. Without supplied sync points, all three proposed both edges within 250 ms of each other for 41 of the 53 missing visits. For M33 on its first pass the starts ranged from 82.64 to 82.68 s and the ends from 84.40 to 84.48 s. This is agreement between related estimates. The performance has no verified timing reference. In particular, the unpinned estimates ended around 345 s despite further musical attacks around 355 s. Inspecting the actual low-D and A attacks also gave better opening landmarks (about 1.3 and 4.75 s) than relying on the repeated pitch pattern alone. On Carry My Name, 36 of the 39 visits where the experiments agreed within 400 ms had both edges within 250 ms of its independent reference.

The proposed next step is an explicit **Continuous performance** draft option. Use credible score/recording landmarks on either side of each uncertain passage, follow local tempo and check note attacks across the passage. Allow leading and trailing video time and score rests. Check the first attack, final chord and repeated passages separately. Changing the mapped duration of a hold does not create another gradable attack. Keep conflicting regions available for audition and manual pins. Before enabling additional grading, verify sampled downbeats from a rubato performance, within-measure alignment through holds, and regression coverage for lessons with speech and multiple takes. This trial changed no shipped playback or matcher behavior.

## Choosing the final take from the preceding chord progression

Compared one-click Auto-sync with `af8c809` using the original scores and recordings. The matcher received no reference times. The earlier matcher could prefer adjacent bar candidates in a later demonstration while overlooking another performance of the same chord changes closer to the preceding scored phrase. On Chord Scale 1 the jump began at measure 8. On Chord Scales 5 and 8 the whole second variation moved to a later demonstration and displaced the final variation too.

| Final measure | Previous recording time | Updated recording time | Accepted Lesson Bridge time | Absolute start / end error |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Chord Scale 1, M10 | 230.228–233.840 s | 54.200–57.840 s | 54.168–57.780 s | 32 / 60 ms |
| Chord Scale 5, M24 | 163.827–167.680 s | 128.680–131.657 s | 128.720–131.847 s | 40 / 190 ms |
| Chord Scale 8, M16 | 132.307–136.400 s | 111.720–115.040 s | 111.743–115.145 s | 23 / 105 ms |

Across the three lessons, 26 previously placed visits changed performances. Both boundaries moved closer on all 26. Measure 16 of Chord Scale 5 was also recovered. It starts at 97.44 s and the draft stops following the chord at 100.00 s. The reference extends that measure to 107.85 s. The counts with both boundaries within 250 ms rose from 0 to 3, 2 to 17 and 6 to 14 respectively. No mapped time overlapped a reference interval labeled explanation in these three lessons. Combined overlap with unmatched source rests increased from zero to 0.42 s at the edges. The 21 other saved browser controls retained every selected visit and endpoint, including the ringing chords from the preceding update.

Chrome never requested the new search module before Auto-sync. On the three target recordings (158–239 s), three paired runs per lesson alternated the old and new builds. Median time from clicking Auto-sync to receiving the worker's choices, including local media decoding, was 1.57 → 1.64 s, 1.74 → 1.73 s and 0.99 → 1.03 s respectively. These are local timings with ordinary run-to-run variation. Future search changes should repeat the same load check, per-lesson timing and boundary comparison. Streaming download time and other devices were not benchmarked.

## Ringing chords at the start and end of a lesson

Compared the one-click browser matcher with `5b68fac` on the 20 previously tested lessons and four more saved Open D recordings (Chord Scales 1, 5, 7 and 8). Chrome received only the original score and recording. The accepted Lesson Bridge timing supplied the reference after analysis. Both the raw selections and the measure visits used by the draft editor were compared.

| Lesson and boundary | Previous | Updated | Reference | Absolute error before → after |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Chord Scale 2, first attack | 19.640 s | 18.800 s | 18.758 s | 882 → 42 ms |
| Chord Scale 2, final release | 155.177 s | 155.560 s | 155.660 s | 483 → 100 ms |
| Chord Scale 1, first attack | 17.640 s | 15.560 s | 15.451 s | 2,189 → 109 ms |

In Chord Scale 2 the opening pitch match landed inside a chord already ringing. The final strum sequence estimated the release from the preceding tempo while the recorded chord persisted under another sound. Within a short window the matcher now checks for an earlier attack that develops into the same sustained chord. At an extrapolated ending it can follow the fading chord through brief masking until it observes the chord disappear. An experiment that reconsidered existing final matches moved Quartal Harmony's already accurate ending. The shipped release check applies only to a freshly extrapolated final chord.

Across 457 selected measure visits, all interior points and selected visit identities stayed the same. Only the three boundaries above moved. In Chord Scale 2 all 21 visits remain selected and 15 now have both boundaries within 250 ms (previously 13). Chord Scale 7's final estimate stayed within 164 ms. Chord Scales 1, 5 and 8 still select a later occurrence for their final measure. Those errors need better take selection. These are automated recording and reference comparisons. Saved profiles are not rewritten.

## Recovering melodies and chord changes from neighboring measures

The November 23 blind rerun gave Auto-sync the original scores and recordings. It used the accepted Lesson Bridge plans only to score the finished choices. The prior benchmark at `31a1d1e` supplied the baseline. A measure visit passes the boundary check when both its start and end are within 250 ms of the reference.

| Case | Placed before → after | Both boundaries within 250 ms before → after |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Carry My Name solo | 13 → 40 of 41 | 7 → 35 |
| Chord Scale 2 | 20 → 21 of 21 | 4 → 13 |
| Chord Scale 6 | 21 → 22 of 24 | 5 → 8 |
| Harlito | 15 → 19 of 25 | 10 → 14 |
| All 12 baseline lessons | 211 → 244 of 279 | 135 → 179 |

In Carry My Name, ten independently selected downbeats agreed on a tempo while the original search missed much of the lead over the backing track. The new search recovered all 40 sounding visits. The silent opening measure stayed unmapped. At measure 25, the GPX calls for an F-sharp bent up a whole step. The recording contains the initial pitch and sustained higher pitch. The draft moved that measure from 41.47 seconds to 48.47 seconds against the reference's 48.48. Measures 25–27 had all been over two seconds early. The largest remaining endpoint error is the last release at 0.52 seconds early.

Chord Scale 2 has several chord sequences with the same ringing D and A. Matching the changing voices and the series of strums corrected measures 8–10 and 13, recognized the held chord across measure 17, and moved measures 18–21 to the later demonstration. Measure 18 now starts at 144.480 seconds against 144.534, previously 110.866. All 21 visits were placed, 19 starts were within 250 ms and all starts were within one second. The recording still overlaps intervals marked as unmatched source rests by 0.80 seconds, down from 19.94. The first downbeat is 0.88 seconds late and the last release is 0.48 seconds early. A draft remains editable where the played release and the written duration differ.

Compared each old and new selected endpoint for the 12 lessons and checked the normalized visits used by the draft editor. All previously placed visits remained and none of their start or end errors increased. Overlap with labeled explanations stayed at 5.98 seconds across the sample. Eight of the 12 lessons retained all endpoints. The five earlier controls (Connecting Intervals, Low Bass Note, Quartal Harmony, Jazz Discovery ii–V–I and Devil Inside) also retained every selection and endpoint in separate one-click browser runs.

After freezing the changes, repeated the old and new browser runs on three additional accepted lessons. Chord Scale 4 improved from 16/20 to 20/20 visits, and from two to eight with both boundaries within 250 ms. Its unmatched-rest overlap fell from 1.94 to 0.02 seconds. Twelve-Eight Wave and Workout 5 Play-Along retained their selections and times. These are automated timing comparisons. The references can contain a duration judgment for a ringing chord or a rest. No listening or physical MIDI acceptance was assumed.

## Blind Auto-sync comparison across five TrueFire courses

On November 23, 2026, the published trainer code at `4f94bc0` ran one-click Auto-sync on 12 score/recording pairs from five courses outside the previous five-lesson test set. Lesson Bridge had 95 original-score pairs accepted by its native timing check. For 58 of those, a manifest named for the lesson contained a recording link. All 12 selected links returned playable MP4 files directly. The test checked the original score hash, compact plan hash, timing-file hash for the selected manifest recording, and recording duration (within two seconds of rounded metadata). Isolated Chrome loaded the original score and recording, used the default practice track and saved the worker's selections. The reference plan was used to evaluate those selections by score measure and repeat occurrence. This is a deliberately varied sample, so its percentages should not be extrapolated to the full catalog.

| Lesson | Reference visits | Placed | Both boundaries within 250 ms | Starts over 2 s off |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Major & Minor Chord Barre (49458) | 13 | 13 | 5 | 2 |
| Chord Scale 2 (49465) | 21 | 20 | 4 | 8 |
| Chord Scale 6 (49473) | 24 | 21 | 5 | 5 |
| Groove 90 (87761) | 20 | 19 | 16 | 0 |
| Harlito (87764) | 25 | 15 | 10 | 0 |
| Bends, Slides & Vibrato (97220) | 15 | 8 | 4 | 2 |
| Carry My Name solo (97237) | 41 | 13 | 7 | 3 |
| Workout 2 lesson (97313) | 24 | 22 | 17 | 1 |
| Workout 2 play-along (97314) | 17 | 15 | 8 | 0 |
| Workout 10 play-along (97333) | 19 | 16 | 13 | 0 |
| The Ionian Mode (98098) | 26 | 24 | 23 | 0 |
| Soloing With the Ionian Mode (98099) | 34 | 25 | 23 | 0 |
| **Total** | **279** | **211** | **135** | **21** |

167 starts and 150 ends were within 250 ms. Across 68 omitted visits, only three had an available candidate within 250 ms of the reference start. Across all 279 visits, 101 had no such candidate in the worker's final list and 11 had one that the default selection missed. Candidate generation and retention are therefore the first places to investigate. For Carry My Name, 28 visits were omitted. Fourteen had sounding score probes and empty candidate lists. Another eleven had candidates whose starts were all outside tolerance, two had close alternatives, and one had no sounding probes.

Selected spans overlapped 5.98 seconds labeled as explanations and another 19.94 seconds labeled as unmatched source rests. Those labels are from Bridge. An unmatched source rest does not identify the audio as speech. On Chord Scale 2, measure 18 started at 110.87 seconds against the reference's 144.53 seconds, in a long unmatched source rest. Use that passage to test whether support on both sides of a long break justifies the selected take. Across the two written-repeat play-alongs, 14 of 15 later occurrences were placed, 13 starts were within 250 ms, and no selected midpoint landed in a different numbered occurrence of the same measure. This check cannot identify an extra demonstration inside unscored time.

The next experiment should trace where the correct phrase disappears in Carry My Name and test a wider candidate search before changing selection thresholds. Compare coverage, boundary error and gap overlap together. Keep lessons from additional courses out of tuning for an independent check. Private recordings, reference plans and per-visit captures stay in the local test workspace.

## Checking a delayed downbeat after a nearly shared bar line

In Quartal Harmony (98109), the two selected estimates almost touched at the end of measure 10 (124.024 seconds) and start of measure 11 (124.056 seconds). The previous check skipped gaps below 0.16 seconds. The tail of measure 10 still agreed with the written final chord shortly before that estimate, and the expected sustained chord of measure 11 had a stronger attack at 124.400 seconds. After the two context searches move both visits, a small positive gap before a single sustained chord can use the same attack check. This additional check searches 0.16 to 0.50 seconds after the proposed downbeat and requires evidence for both chords.

Blind Auto-sync moved the shared boundary to 124.400 seconds against the publisher’s 124.447, reducing error from 423 ms to 47 ms. Quartal Harmony’s measures 8 and 9 retained both endpoints, and measure 10 still begins at 121.520 seconds. Only the end of 10 and the start of 11 moved. The end of 11 remains 1.39 seconds early. Connecting Intervals, Low Bass Note, ii–V–I Major Scale and Devil Inside retained every selected visit and endpoint. Tested the original GPX/video pairs in isolated Chrome and added a synthetic delayed-downbeat regression alongside the local recording check.

Keep Quartal Harmony as a focused regression for repeated takes, sustained chords and spoken gaps. A listening pass through measures 8–11 can check how the boundaries feel in practice. For further tuning, first compare lessons from other courses. The saved Lesson Bridge original-timing inventory supplies 95 native-accepted pairs with local scores and matching saved plan hashes. The plans include 29 pairs with repeat visits and 72 with marked gaps. Those figures describe reference pairs. The scan found no standalone lesson recordings inside the Bridge evidence folder. Each new blind run needs the same playable recording and version as its saved reference. Use accepted pairs to measure coverage, exact repeat-visit selection, boundary error and explanation overlap. Keep a set of different courses out of tuning and include review-only examples separately to exercise uncertain timing.

## Verifying bar lines after a repeated take moves

Quartal Harmony (98109) measure 8 has one written chord. The ordinary scan selected a short match at 58.900 seconds during an explanation. The seven-measure route also carried the chord near the new measure 9, while the five-measure route omitted measure 8. After a contested take moves, the worker can use this longer candidate if a separate local candidate overlaps its start and the audio confirms the written chords at both ends. For nearby consecutive moved bars, the worker checks for the last chord of the first bar at the old cutoff and uses the next downbeat attack as their shared boundary when the provisional gap is 0.16 to 0.50 seconds. The attack check requires a sustained energy rise and agreement with the expected chord in the practice track’s register. The prior takes remain available.

Blind Auto-sync moved measure 8’s start from 58.900 to 111.120 seconds (publisher 111.262, 142 ms early). Measure 9 runs from 115.960 to 121.520 seconds (publisher 116.049 to 121.638, 89 ms and 118 ms early). The previous end of measure 9 was 120.829 seconds. Measure 10 starts on the shared boundary and keeps its previous end. The new take no longer overlaps the publisher’s explanation intervals. The method anchors attacks and does not classify speech within an otherwise timed measure.

Isolated Chrome applied one-click Auto-sync from the original GPX/video pairs with the published timing withheld. Quartal Harmony changed only measures 8–10. Connecting Intervals, Low Bass Note, the ii–V–I Major Scale lesson, and Devil Inside kept every selected visit and both endpoints. Synthetic regressions cover an unsupported preceding chord, an attack with the wrong pitch, and a longer gap. The optional Quartal Harmony recording regression checks all three boundaries against the publisher. Physical MIDI and audible playback remain local checks.

## Choosing a repeated take from its neighboring measures

The original three-measure scan and both gap recovery passes still produce the first draft. When that pitch-based route has an internal omission and competing takes, the worker also searches five- and seven-measure windows in the practice track’s pitch range. Both searches use the GPX playback order and decoded recording. Adjacent visits must agree on a different performance before either longer search can change a choice. An isolated hit or a passage supported only by the same single-bar guesses supplies no vote. The final selection weights these agreements in playback order, keeps ordinary timing where the performances coincide and retains every original take for review.

On Quartal Harmony in Lydian Mode (98109), simply adding five- or seven-measure windows over the whole mix kept measure 9 at 60.760 seconds. Searching the instrument register with single-window replacement gave 117.020 seconds with five measures and 116.211 seconds with seven. The combined selection used 116.211–120.829 seconds. TrueFire gives 116.049–121.638. The start is 163 ms late and the end is 809 ms early. Of 45 visits, 35 are now selected and 26 starts are within 250 ms of the publisher (previously 22 selected and zero within 250 ms). Two selected midpoints are outside their intended visits, including measure 8 in a published explanation. Summed overlap of selected measures with published breaks fell from 16.41 seconds to 1.40 seconds, all in measure 8.

On Connecting Intervals (87755), unconstrained replacement with five measures lost three previously quarter-second starts and seven measures lost two. The combined selection preserves all 18 formerly quarter-second starts. Measure 9 moved from 377.267 to 416.232 seconds (reference 416.270), and measure 10 moved from 379.367 to 421.680 (reference 421.953). Measures 13–15 lose their conflicting selections. All other starts and ends, including 26, are unchanged. Selected visits went from 30 to 27, starts within 250 ms from 18 to 19, and starts within one second from 21 to 23. None of the 27 selected midpoints lies in a published explanation. Measure 24 extends 1.41 seconds into a published break in both versions.

Isolated Chrome ran one-click Auto-sync with the original five GPX/video pairs and no publisher timing supplied. Reviewed timing applied on all five. Every selected visit and both endpoints remained identical on Low Bass Note (17), the ii–V–I Major Scale lesson (18), and Devil Inside (12). Counts refer to these recordings, which informed development. Optional local regression tests replay the saved Quartal Harmony and Connecting Intervals worker inputs. Listening and physical MIDI were outside this comparison.

## Auto-sync across several missing measures, November 22, 2026

Auto-sync uses the end of the previous selected visit and the start of the next as pins for up to four missing visits. Both neighboring tempos must fit the duration within 15%. The existing pinned passage matcher places interior bar lines. Each estimated bar must stay within 20% of its relative score duration at the passage tempo and independently match the pitches in its register. Short articulations can qualify through at least four onset probes spanning the bar. A failed bar leaves the whole run open. This pass preserves existing selections and competing takes.

Five original GPX/video pairs ran in isolated Chrome with the production worker. Only Jazz Discovery 98027 gained selections (15 to 18 of 20). The new starts for measures 15–17 were 74.653, 76.767 and 78.953 seconds. Relative to the publisher these were 175 ms late, 51 ms early and 87 ms early. The end at 81.267 seconds was 48 ms early. The 175 ms offset comes from the existing end of measure 14. The start of 18 also remained fixed. One-click Auto-sync activated practice. Simulated MIDI scored all 113 attacks, including 6, 6 and 9 in the recovered bars.

Connecting Intervals (30 selected), Low Bass Note (17), Quartal Harmony (22) and Devil Inside (12) retained their prior selections and times. Across the first three and Jazz Discovery, withholding runs of two to four previously selected visits recovered 51 of 124 overlapping test windows. All 51 fell in the published visits, including 21 second-pass windows on Low Bass Note. Recovery by lesson was 18/27 for Jazz Discovery, 33/39 for Low Bass Note, 0/37 for Connecting Intervals and 0/21 for Quartal Harmony. Overlapping windows are correlated. Zero of 102 test windows crossing a documented break and zero of 4,671 constructed opportunities within publisher-unscored intervals were filled. These checks exercise the combined tempo, score and audio gates. They do not classify all unscored audio as speech. Quartal Harmony still selects an earlier take for measure 9. Sparse chord passages and incorrect outer pins need separate evidence.

The original recording for the older Breaking Down 7th Arpeggios benchmark was absent from its saved fixture. A fresh run on that pair and physical MIDI/listening checks remain open. The optional tests in `tests/audio-sync-saved-passages.test.js` replay Jazz Discovery and a withheld Low Bass Note second pass when the local worker captures are available. Generated regression audio covers accepted and rejected runs without those files.

## Matching a measure between two synced measures, November 22, 2026

On Jazz Discovery Vol. 2 lesson 98027 the original browser worker selected 14 of 20 visits. Measure 9 had three plausible takes elsewhere in the recording. It had no candidate between the end of measure 8 and the start of measure 10, which was itself found by the previous-measure search. The old search skipped occupied candidate lists and did not revisit this new pair of neighbors. The mix also favors other pitches over the guitar part during measure 9.

The worker now checks isolated holes after the backward pass. Both neighbors must predict a duration within 15% of their respective local tempos. A second FFT over the gap tests the written pitch sequence in the track’s register with a five-semitone margin. The same pitch evidence checks used by the backward pass must succeed, including support in both halves and a better match than a sustained harmony. This pass adds a take at the two existing boundaries and keeps earlier choices and alternatives.

With GPX and MP4 alone, Auto-sync selected measure 9 at 60.7533–62.9840 s. TrueFire’s separately retrieved reference gives 60.7250–63.0502 s. Chrome Realtime credited all 92 attacks in 15 selected visits, including the four new attacks in measure 9. The remaining unscored interval added zero wrong notes from injected MIDI. A separate manual two-pin replay over measures 15–17 gave internal starts of 76.767 and 78.953 s, 51 and 87 ms early. That three-measure result comes from the existing refiner.

Connecting Intervals, Low Bass Note, and Quartal Harmony kept their former 30, 17 and 22 automatic selections. Across all four lessons, withholding one selected measure with two selected neighbors recovered 11 of 54. All 11 midpoints fell in the reference visit. Zero of 453 tempo-matched challenges drawn from publisher-unscored audio passed. These controls cover sampled recordings. They do not establish general speech detection. Quartal Harmony’s selected measure 9 is a separate take-selection issue (60.76 s selected, 116.05 s in the reference). Physical MIDI and listening remain owner checks.

Other routes to playback-only timing include a missing endpoint, a recording time outside the file, overlapping music or contradictory anchors, an invalid repeat/pass assignment, or an unverified silent omission. A score or recording identity mismatch prevents applying the timing. A gap on its own does not prevent grading placed attacks.

Blind rerun of the production worker on the same GPX and recording hashes with the saved measure/pass timings withheld. Connecting Intervals kept 30 of 40 selections. Low Bass Note recovered 16 of 17, including all eight second passes. Second-pass start errors ranged from 0.57 s early to 0.49 s late. None of these 18 recovered spans intersected a reference gap or landed on another take by midpoint. This measures recovery when the following downbeat has been selected. The test start was chosen to exercise editing.

## Automatic timing on a riff lesson

The *Devil Inside* intro had a known music start but no labeled explanation interval. Skip now uses musical coverage. In Chrome, the player jumped from 0:12.5 to 1:11.64 while paused and from an internal gap to 3:20.28 during playback. Pausing that second take previously snapped the recording to the first take. The host now retains recording time across native Pause and resume with a timing plan. A simulated read error exercised Play-triggered reload. The intermittent failure in the owner’s original tab remains unconfirmed.

A separate browser check reproduced a misleading pre-review audition. At 71.85 seconds, the selected take belonged to the opening pickup while the ordinary recording cursor was on the second written bar, pass 2. Take auditions now project the chosen visit from its proposed recording bounds and stop at its end.

The earlier eight timestamp comparisons did not test score labels or full coverage. The GPX marks its opening riff bar as a pickup. The next printed measure is 1. In draft playback the cursor reached the correct first two bars but the editor showed M1 and M2. Media Sync now labels them M0 and M1. The section marked 4:34 has no selected passage. Draft review describes this audio as unmapped.

On November 22, the original automatic search returned zero candidates for a 415.92-second riff lesson. The single-bar scores peaked at 0.677 against a 0.78 cutoff. The best opening three-bar alignment scored 0.485 against 0.52. A fallback now compares pitch at the expected note times over four sounding measures and tests each measure against its own average pitch profile. Written rests preserve the spacing to the next note. Whole silent visits receive no suggested points.

Chrome loaded the GPX and MP4 with no pins, printed time cues, or reference timing supplied to the matcher. Review opened a draft with 12 selected sounding visits. Four opening starts and four outro starts compared with independently supplied Soundslice entries each differed by less than 60 ms. The other four starts belong to a slower take at 200.28 seconds with no corresponding reference entries. The score expands to 40 visits while the timing file has 16 entries including boundaries. Several written passes share a riff. The eight comparisons verify timing for those passages, while pass attribution and full-lesson alignment still require listening. This lesson informed development of the fallback.

## Pinned passage comparison across three lessons

With the correct passage and exact outer pins, the existing browser matcher, trackswitch and equal spacing respectively placed 16/17, 17/17 and 17/17 interior starts within 250 ms on a repeated guitar exercise. Both matchers placed all eight second-pass starts within that tolerance across seven pin settings. On two interrupted lessons the exact-pin counts were 18/31, 21/31 and 18/31, and 48/56, 50/56 and 18/56. Errors use all reference interior starts as the denominator. Pins came from reference passage boundaries; these are assisted refinement results.

A second original browser alignment works at written tempo and can sustain a score or recording frame across a longer pause. When it offers a materially different interior boundary the player can hear either start and change the shared edge of two adjacent selected measures. Both passage pins remain fixed. Trials with timing penalties and local smoothing gave mixed results. The UI retains the existing automatic choice. Chrome checks cover listening, selection changes and handing the adjusted pair to the draft editor.

## Passage timing drafts — 2026-11-22

Media Sync searches up to three played measures at once and ranks candidates in playback order. The continuous-passage editor pins both recording edges and uses an original pitch-and-attack alignment to propose the interior points. No external matcher code or model is bundled.

Synthetic regressions cover independent takes, ordered repeats, silence, explanation gaps and a tempo change inside exact pins. The Chrome workflow exercises playhead pins, invalid ranges, a repeated take, review, undo, accepted gap playback, local files, readable links and denied reads.

## Audio sync suggestions — 2026-11-21

Implemented an original browser matcher using pitch-class passage search. Local analysis proposes candidate times for each native measure occurrence. The lesson owner chooses takes and reviews gaps before activating the timing. No external library or service is required. [AudioLabs passage-search background](https://www.audiolabs-erlangen.de/resources/MIR/FMP/C7/C7S2_AudioMatching.html)

`node --test tests/audio-sync.test.js` exercises known-time synthesized phrases, slower and extra takes, written repeat order, gaps and no-match inputs. `node tests/run-audio-sync-browser.cjs` covers local files, direct URLs, denied reads, cancellation, auditions, take selection, draft undo and reviewed playback in Chrome. Real lesson accuracy remains an owner listening check.

## Activating a reviewed passage

The owner can now accept a draft directly after listening. The action states whether the result grades placed measures, follows the reviewed cursor with grading off, or plays a recording with no mapped measures. The local playback choice retains the timestamps and opens paused. The original evidence and working draft remain available for export. Incoming verified plans still require native validation.

Use reviewed timing can activate individually placed GPX passages after review. Gaps have no practice targets. Fill 1 exposed a second blocker when its first measure consumed zero ticks. The reader now recognizes its explicit silent pickup, and the native clock uses the next played measure at tick zero. A browser check clicked Use reviewed timing and Play with both a newly prepared draft and the earlier passage suggestions.

## Recording timing with repeat signs and an open final endpoint

The score adapter now reads qualified one-bar and paired two-bar donors, including ordinary pick direction. Replayed notes supply positive attacks and local release evidence when the donor is fully bounded. Unsupported donor effects and genuine ties across a recording break retain the recording guard. The draft editor can place a missing last endpoint at the loaded media duration on explicit request. Native simile tests cover both donors, malformed pairs and positive attacks. These are application changes outside the bundled alphaTab reader.

## Bakersfield draft timing review

The timing compiler accepts an explicitly omitted first pickup with zero native ticks and full silence across all staves. The native score adapter recognizes ordinary vibrato on a fully traced within-measure tie. Cross-break ties and unproved holds retain their guards. Both changes live in Trainer application code. The bundled alphaTab and its updater require no patch. Regression coverage is in score-rests and tie-hold-boundary.

## Generated let-ring releases at recording breaks — 2026-11-20

A private two-guitar lesson reproduced a disabled **Use reviewed timing** after review notices were cleared. The Electric guitar released G3 and E3 exactly at the start of the final spoken breakdown. The hold estimator had extended both notes to later attacks. Trainer now uses uniquely paired generated MIDI releases for otherwise plain let-ring notes and reciprocal ties that end within the same measure. The two-guitar draft activated with all 51 visits through Scan, Send, Clear reviewed notices and Use reviewed timing in isolated Chrome with silent test audio. Regressions use generated scores and include late or missing releases, overlapping pitches, immediate reattacks, pedal and cross-break ties.

Waveform availability appears in the timeline and recording failures share the recovery row with Reload recording. Profile state sits beside Save.

The Loop menu labels the step **Speed increase (%)**. A value of 5 takes playback from 60% to 65% of the score tempo.

Speed Trainer lives inside Loop and uses the existing Realtime synth transport. The selected range repeats at the target until the player stops. Wait retains its independent metronome tempo. Media Sync adds one Drag / nudge choice for stretching the selected timestamps against their final marker. Undo and Redo cover the entire edit. Both features are opt-in and use the existing score and profile formats.

## Written bend ranges — 2026-11-15

Owner authorized guitar-specific note acceptance for bends, pre-bends and whammy, including chords. **Allow written bend ranges** starts on for fresh and earlier preferences, persists independently, and uses the existing pause/clear-results change behavior. Off restores original exact onset matching. Existing MIDI tolerance defaults, practice timing, pitch-only grading and native score/playback behavior remain unchanged.

Written base pitch and the performed tie chain's curve range can satisfy each affected note. Fine ranges are not rounded out to the next semitone. Simultaneous bend/whammy curves are combined at shared time points; overlapping chord ranges retain separate requirements and can reassign earlier accepted attacks so strum order does not prevent completion. One attack still fills one requirement. Broad whammy ranges are forgiving by design; acceptance does not verify endpoint, direction, shape, duration or technique quality.

Vibrato does not widen a new note's pitch requirement. Continuous pitch-wheel messages never create attacks. An always-on bounded guard associates a unique released pitch change with a previously accepted expressive note within 60 ms after release; Hex also recognizes an unreleased same-channel replacement. Its lifetime uses the source duration at playback speed, with a 200 ms minimum and one-second fallback. Vibrato re-keys must stay within one semitone of the accepted base. The guard never widens the initial pitch requirement and remains active with range acceptance off. Contradictory attacks and expired Together attempts discard the associated evidence. This can still mistake exceptionally quick separate notes for continuation. Pause, seeks/new sessions and configuration discard gesture evidence; raw MIDI remains available in capture alongside the setting, range and acceptance evidence. Visual acceptance stays attached to the written note without inventing a detected physical fret.

Validation: all 671 Node tests pass, including native classic/core curve normalization and 91 grading cases. Isolated Chrome passes the new written-bend runner in all six practice/input combinations, tolerance defaults/persistence/desktop-phone layout, and existing Realtime written-window/late-chord feedback checks. Changed modules pass syntax checks and git diff whitespace checks. The sandbox blocked the local-server test's loopback connection; it passed with the full suite outside that network restriction. Physical Fishman/Poly acceptance remains pending; compare bends, pre-bends, releases, fine bends, whammy, vibrato, chord order, ties and fast separate attacks in both practice modes. ## Display-only score text spacing — 2026-11-05

Native springs improve this; a 480-unscaled-pixel minimum canvas for labeled scores also prevents native fitting from squeezing a single measure excessively at phone widths/high zoom. It scales with zoom and uses the existing panel's horizontal scrolling, without page overflow. No measures-per-line control, score rewriting or general engraving changes were needed. The trainer-owned SVG engine uses the main-thread native renderer so screen and native print share the adapter; `core.useWorkers` and native synth/audio workers stay enabled. Verification: 498 Node tests pass. New `run-text-layout-browser.cjs` passes 108 screen cases and three native print previews for saved GPX, Soundslice-derived GP and an original dense two-track GP fixture: widths 390/600/1600, zoom 75/100/150/200, notation/TAB/both. ## Realtime written-span forgiveness — 2026-10-29

Owner confirmed Realtime-only scope and approved accepting attacks throughout each written target span, retaining early grace and allowing chord tones to accumulate within that span. Earlier fixed 160 ms late/60 ms chord-spread behavior is superseded. Current unfinished pitches take precedence over early repeated occurrences; only the immediate upcoming onset can receive 120 ms early credit. Exact end boundaries belong to the next target/rest. No arbitrary additional late grace extends into rests. Invalid/missing written durations retain a bounded legacy fallback.

Changes are confined to pure timing/session projection, target end metadata and recording-time projection. Native alphaTab transport/rendering, MIDI normalization and tolerance/correction rules, Wait, score colors, fretboard missed-target suppression and held-note gesture evidence remain intact. Widened explicit chord collection deliberately permits slower strums/arpeggiation within the target, not across later attacks. No new runtime modules, saved preferences or version bump. ## First-use permission send survives popup closure — 2026-10-28

Owner confirms the extension loads/works, but first Send requests access and then does nothing; reopening/retrying works after grants persist. Code inspection confirms all prior Send work lived in the dismissible popup. Chrome documents automatic popup closure on outside focus; the exact owner's prompt dismissal sequence is not directly reproduced. Moved Send into `send-worker.js` and `send-job.js`; no trainer app files changed. Worker starts permission request directly in the incoming click-message event before storage awaits, rejects concurrent sends and owns download/delivery even if the response recipient disappears. Only this extension's popup sender is accepted. Decline stops before downloads. Matching remains exact origin/path with exactly one app tab. Score/timing downloads run concurrently within the existing 25-second request window, keeping work bounded between extension API calls. Delivery is one-shot, with no automatic retries. App review/save/autoplay rules are unchanged.

The manifest now declares the module worker and minimum Chrome 116 (permission-prompt lifetime support). Only short send status is stored in chrome.storage.session; URL-bearing errors are replaced before storing. A restarted worker reports an interrupted pending send rather than replaying it. Selection/score/timing stay only in memory until completion. Popup restores the latest status on reopen. Preview/Test/Inspect remain popup-scoped. Privacy, README, module responsibilities and store disclosures now distinguish this on-demand worker from background browsing monitoring. ZIP allowlist includes both worker modules. Source folder stays the unpacked installation; dist is only the generated store archive.

Verification: all 480 Node tests pass on Node 24. New worker regression explicitly destroys the reply recipient while approval is pending, then verifies one delivery/focus and reopened status; also covers immediate permission invocation, decline/no download, duplicate in-flight Send, foreign sender rejection, missing/duplicate destination and no replay after restart. Existing synthetic popup browser runner passes through the shared send job with mocked Chrome APIs. A fresh isolated real Chrome process loaded the unpacked extension using the test-only Extensions.loadUnpacked API and started send-worker.js from the actual popup; this checks packaging/startup, not approval of a real permission prompt. Syntax and whitespace checks pass; rebuilt ZIP byte comparisons verify its files against source. Normal browser/Live Server and Fishman were not touched. Owner still needs one fresh-grant/decline check in normal Chrome. No store upload/submission, app version/tag or grading/MIDI changes.

References: [popup lifecycle](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/develop/ui/add-popup), [Chrome service-worker lifecycle](https://developer.chrome.com/docs/extensions/develop/concepts/service-workers/lifecycle).

## Media ruler and compact editor — 2026-10-28

Owner reports improved media sync but blurred timestamps at high zoom. The previous SVG expanded to hundreds of thousands of pixels; browser raster limits could degrade text despite counter-scaling. The new viewport-sized sticky SVG uses a logical-width spacer and moving viewBox. Ruler spacing uses round time intervals; only visible ticks and markers are drawn. ## All Of Me origin-dependent YouTube result — 2026-10-28

Further investigation identifies a concrete condition, superseding the earlier unresolved diagnosis for current loading. YouTube's normal watch-page player response for EmeenSNRGBc reports status OK and playableInEmbed:true. Its embedded_player_response under PLAYER_VARS instead returns previewPlayabilityStatus UNPLAYABLE with only “This video is unavailable” when embedded from http://127.0.0.1:8081. Both official youtube.com and youtube-nocookie.com embed hosts reproduce that failure; the same-creator control 2K_sBujnYfo returns OK. A direct navigation to /embed without a parent/referrer returned 153 for both and is not valid evidence about this 150 failure.

Controlled in-memory parent pages (no deployed files or altered repository code) change only the parent address: http://localhost:8081 returns preview status OK for EmeenSNRGBc, and a test page at the existing https://ztbishop.github.io origin also returns OK. Actual current Guitar Trainer on the existing Live Server via http://localhost:8081 then loads the supplied All-Of-Me-ChM.gp and exact failing video successfully in two fresh isolated Chrome runs. An explicit Preview click reports playing:true and a recording clock advancing to approximately 2168 ms. No API origin spoofing, credentials, browser settings, vendor changes or alternate video were used. Owner's normal browser/server remained untouched. This is load/clock evidence, not listening or Fishman acceptance.

Conclusion: the current failure is reproducibly dependent on the numeric-IP embedding origin for this video, not a global disabled-embedding flag. YouTube does not expose the internal policy/reason for that origin distinction, or its history, so do not attribute it to a creator decision/copyright claim or assert which address the owner used previously. Recommend opening the same Live Server at localhost:8081 and loading the original file/link there. localhost and 127.0.0.1 have separate browser libraries/preferences; existing data stays at its original address, with existing Backup Library/Import Library for deliberate transfer. No automatic redirect or storage migration is added. Documentation-only update; diff check and actual browser observations are the relevant verification.

## Deferred MusicXML presentation fidelity investigation — 2026-10-26

Owner requested investigation of chord-frame finger numbers, simultaneous above/below direction words and slash-chord bass in Soundslice Tool exports, then explicitly chose to record the findings for a possible later revisit. **Investigation only; implementation is deferred.** No importer adapter, annotation overlay, numbered-diagram renderer, native/vendor change, upgrade or synthetic regression was added. Existing unfinished extension-store packaging changes were present and remain outside this documentation update. README needs no feature announcement because behavior has not changed.

Dependency evidence: assets/vendor/alphatab/vendor-manifest.json pins @coderline/alphatab 1.8.4. The official npm latest endpoint also returned 1.8.4 during this investigation. Current upstream develop source was inspected, not installed; it still lacks per-string chord-frame finger storage and the relevant harmony bass handling. Recheck versions/source if revisiting; neither a future fix nor upgrade compatibility is established. The existing documented vendor string-line guard is unrelated and was not changed.

Confirmed limitations in the pinned engine:

- MusicXmlImporter._parseHarmonyFrame reads frame strings, frets, first fret and barres but ignores frame-note/fingering. model.Chord has no per-string finger-number collection. Its showFingering flag does not establish support for storing/rendering these source labels; ChordDiagramGlyph renders fret/open/muted indicators and filled dots, without source finger numerals. Import-only data repair cannot make the native diagram draw them.
- _parseDirection retains one pending _nextBeatText string; later words can overwrite earlier words before the next beat. The model exposes one Beat.text field and TextEffectInfo uses an above-staff text band. The importer does not preserve source above/below placement as independent annotations. TextGlyph supports multiline text, but joining strings alone would not restore original placement. Offset/staff/voice anchoring would also need independent verification.
- _parseHarmony reads root/kind/degrees/frame and ignores bass. Slash-bass text could plausibly be restored through the supported chord-name model, without modifying note pitches or grading. Correct source-to-beat/chord matching, accidentals, repeated chord definitions and paired-part import still require original synthetic regressions.

Local verification used existing files in the owner's separate Soundslice Tool/private folder, read directly without copying them into this repository: life-of-a-fool-visible-frames-DIAGNOSTIC.musicxml (272 frame finger labels), lesson7-checked.musicxml (concurrent upper/lower direction words), and Soundslice engine validation/01 - Let It Snow.musicxml (six harmony bass entries). A fresh isolated Chrome profile and this app's loopback server exercised normal Open File and Keep separate. Native chord/model inspection confirmed absent finger-number storage and omitted slash bass. Actual trainer SVG text in Lesson 7 showed the lower instruction above the score and omitted the simultaneous upper instruction. One initial inspection assumed every staff had a chord map; the corrected null-safe read completed. This is automated model/DOM/SVG evidence, not proof of faithful engraving, listening, physical MIDI or an implemented fix. No private score, signed URL, capture, generated lesson fixture or screenshot was committed.

Proposed continuation, **not approved or implemented**: first use original synthetic MusicXML to test a narrowly scoped slash-bass adapter and preservation of direction/frame metadata. An app-owned presentation layer would need a separate feasibility trial for numbered diagrams and true above/below annotation lanes; public native model fields alone do not provide these layouts. Keep runtime native integration in js/alphatab-host.js and parsing/projection separate. Do not patch the vendor bundle, infer fingers, relabel unsupported placement as supported, add musical events or alter grading. Check ordinary and paired imports, offset/voice/staff anchoring, original/library bytes, zoom, wrapping, track visibility, native redraw and chord-display choices. Compare generated targets/timing before and after, then verify actual rendered results with synthetic fixtures and the local private diagnostics. Layout collisions and exact publisher engraving remain unresolved risks.

References inspected: [official npm latest metadata](https://registry.npmjs.org/@coderline%2Falphatab/latest), upstream [MusicXmlImporter](https://github.com/CoderLine/alphaTab/blob/develop/packages/alphatab/src/importer/MusicXmlImporter.ts), [Chord model](https://github.com/CoderLine/alphaTab/blob/develop/packages/alphatab/src/model/Chord.ts), [Beat model](https://github.com/CoderLine/alphaTab/blob/develop/packages/alphatab/src/model/Beat.ts) and [ChordDiagramGlyph](https://github.com/CoderLine/alphaTab/blob/develop/packages/alphatab/src/rendering/glyphs/ChordDiagramGlyph.ts). These develop links can change; the pinned local source is the reproducible baseline. For this follow-up, review documentation/diff whitespace only; no runtime change requires a new regression or hardware test run.

Owner acceptance after hard refresh in normal Live Server/browser:
1. Enable a short loop. Open Media Sync, load your video and update Start/End. Preview should play freely; no grading/targets/shading is expected while editing.
2. Select Synth audio, then Finish editing / Return to practice. Playback should pause, the button should say Play, fretboard targets and outside-loop gray shading should return, and the cursor should lie inside the loop. Press Play: only synth audio should sound; play correct/wrong notes and check score, staff/tab and fretboard feedback.
3. Repeat with Recording left selected when finishing. Press Play: recording/cursor should follow alignment, correct/wrong MIDI should grade, and looping should stay in the chosen section. A brief loading pause on source switch is expected; a stuck disabled Play, frozen cursor, absent restored shading/targets, unexpected autoplay, both sources sounding, or persistent absence of grading after Play are failures. Record the source, editor state and steps if one occurs.
4. In Wait after finishing, playback should remain silent/paused until MIDI solves each target; video can step with targets. Reopen/finish a few times and switch sources again.
5. Fresh/private profile defaults video Left. Existing saved placement should remain; change placement and reload to verify persistence. Saved sync profiles can restore their own placement. Hardware, audible alignment/provider behavior and owner appearance remain pending.

## MIDI tolerance across practice modes — 2026-10-21

## Optional brief off-target duration tolerance — 2026-10-20

Owner approved a forgiving Wait-only filter after reviewing MG3 capture counts, then refined it to exempt correct notes from duration rejection. Practice → MIDI input tolerance now includes Ignore brief off-target notes, default off. When enabled, Minimum error duration appears with typed integer entry 5–100 ms and 5 ms arrow steps. Initial duration 30 ms. brief-error-controls.js owns UI validation/sync, captures the clicked value before synchronous stop repaint, and calls bootstrap to stop/reset Wait, save active-device settings and reconfigure. No selected input disables the control with explanatory text. Both settings live in validated gts.midiDevices profiles, independent of global practice settings. Switching/reconnecting/reloading devices restores each profile; turning off retains duration. Old profiles default off/30 without a storage-version bump.

WaitSession still grades correct pitches and percussive targets immediately. Off-target attacks enter pending state with their original record, inferred display metadata, raw channel/pitch and timestamp; they add no score penalty or red feedback until active duration reaches the threshold. Matching releases strictly before the threshold remove them and record ignored-brief-off-target. Sustained/unknown-release attacks count once at or after threshold via the existing controller clock or next MIDI message. Pending note-off matching uses raw channel/pitch (also in Poly), so rotating MPE channels are not treated as physical strings. Histories suppress pending entries from wrong-note projection; noteStatesFromRecords likewise excludes pending errors. Hex bend/spike reconciliation shares removeWrong so counted/pending penalties cannot double-decrement.

Errors remain on their original target even after correct-note advancement or Together retry. Pause freezes active duration; releases while paused use the frozen time. A pending error at the final gate temporarily enters settling until its release or threshold resolves, then finalizes/archives the pass, avoiding dropped loop errors. Correct-note credit is immediate; only final completion/loop handoff may wait for the remaining short window and clock service. Resets/seeks/device changes create new sessions and discard prior pending results consistently with existing reset behavior. Delayed release processing can reconcile a prematurely clock-counted short onset within the current session using the original event timestamp. Native alphaTab transport/layout and Realtime grading are unchanged.

This is explicit tolerance, not reliable ghost-note detection: genuine brief wrong notes are forgiven; ghost notes at the required pitch still earn credit. Raw MIDI capture remains unchanged and now includes ignoreBriefErrors/minErrorMs, initial pending-off-target outcomes and separately recorded briefErrorDecisions in snapshots/history. Missing releases do not imply a short note. Other filters keep their own meanings and saved behavior.

## Christmas MIDI artifact audit and score-range tolerance — 2026-10-19

The owner disputes measure-5 A-string fret 12, measure-10 B-string fret 3 and repeated-measure-5 G-string fret 23. Raw capture rows #119/#313/#433 contain those MIDI pitches, respectively 57/62/78; the app did not infer the raw pitch. Physical fingering is not established by MIDI. #119 has velocity 10 and lasts 1125.1 ms on string 5 while the target is string 1/fret 5. The previous filter requires a unique written pitch on the reported string and a release ≤70 ms, so it cannot remove this event. #313 lasts 2 ms at velocity 114, followed by required MIDI 63 at velocity 116 after 6 ms; it fails the earlier softness/distance bounds. #433 lasts 4 ms at velocity 40, followed by required MIDI 65 at velocity 83 after 13 ms; it fails the earlier velocity cap. hex-tracking-spikes.js retains the original ≤20 velocity/≤70 ms/≥9 semitone/≤200 ms/2× velocity rule and adds a separate released-onset rule: duration ≤5 ms, next correct same-channel written tone ≤20 ms after onset, equal or stronger velocity, same target and retry. This is heuristic reconciliation with explicit reason metadata; no unconditional short-note rejection, octave folding or generated attack. Genuine shorter correct notes still count immediately. Provisional wrong marks can appear before confirmation. Strict projection tests disable tolerance explicitly.

Owner approved a separate score-range option, default off, for Wait Hex and Poly. score-range-filter.js validates tuning/capo/transposition against every target's string/fret/pitch, excludes written open strings from the fretted-shape bounds, expands the shape by five frets in each direction and permits open-string pitches. Hex checks only the configured reported string; Poly ignores only if every possible string lies outside. Written target pitches always pass. Percussive targets, absent/invalid fingering, unsupported tuning metadata and unknown Hex string mapping fail open. Input outside this optional range gets ignored-score-range, no credit/penalty, with an explicit written-score basis and tested positions. This does not read TriplePlay software hand-position information or make Poly position detection claims. It can hide genuine distant mistakes. Preference changes stop/reconfigure Wait and clear results; Realtime is unchanged.

## Confirmed Wait Hex tracking spikes — 2026-10-16

Owner authorized the previously discussed narrow spike rule. Latest capture isolated measure-2 channel-4 MIDI 65 velocity 10, released after 45.1 ms then expected 56 after 192.1 ms; measure-7 channel-4 MIDI 76 velocity 10, released after 58.9 ms then expected 55 after 62.9 ms. The latter also receives an onset bend that updates the wrong sounding pitch to 75; its raw release remains 76. Measure-6 adjacent errors are velocity 127 and about 450 ms; they remain mistakes. Measure-5 velocity-77 / four-semitone error and measure-8 adjacent errors also fail this rule.

hex-tracking-spikes.js defines conservative evidence bounds: wrong onset velocity 1–20, distance at least nine semitones from the unique written tone on that mapped string, captured matching raw Note-Off within 70 ms, next same-channel attack within 200 ms on the same target and Together retry, correct pitch and at least twice the velocity. No release, missing mapping/velocity/time, intervening same-channel attack, changed target/retry, sustained/loud/adjacent error or soft follow-up cannot qualify. WaitSession grades immediately, then reconciles only that retained wrong entry after confirming the whole pattern. It never alters a raw message, synthesizes a correct note, changes a target, adds a fret cap or rejects short correct notes. A provisional red mark can appear until correction. Existing bend correction can update the candidate's wrong entry; a bend-corrected entry cannot be removed twice.

Practice has an independent saved Filter confirmed tracking spikes (Wait Hex) checkbox, default on, disabled outside Wait Hex. Changing it stops Wait and clears its results. This is optional tolerance using configured Hex string identity; Poly including ordinary MIDI Guitar 3 input and Realtime remain unchanged. Pause clears candidates; new attempts/seeks/loops/reconfiguration get fresh sessions. Controller release timestamps now use the same pause-adjusted clock as attacks. Diagnostics retain original wrong rows and raw bytes, add trackingSpike to the confirming outcome and trackingSpikes to retained history/snapshots. No physical fret claim is made.

Existing diagnostic short-error test explicitly disables tolerance to retain its strict capture/control comparison. Changed application modules pass syntax and whitespace checks. ## USB hotplug and duplicate UI work — 2026-10-16

Owner reports full Chrome hangs around updates, then identifies unplugging TriplePlay USB as a possible shared trigger. Investigation found app-side hotplug weaknesses and redundant UI work, not a reproduced full-browser crash. midi-router previously rebuilt UI for every MIDI state notification, notified during partial listener switching, did not explicitly close its old implicitly opened port, and could refresh repeatedly for unchanged/output connection events. Browser/driver fault attribution remains unconfirmed. W3C documents implicit opening when attaching a MIDIMessageEvent listener and connection-state notifications: https://www.w3.org/TR/webmidi/ .

The router now filters disconnected ports, compares input inventory/selected-object identity, ignores output-only and unchanged notifications, and performs one completed device transition. A listener epoch/source guard ignores stale queued messages after detach. Selected input identity remains preferred across disconnect; it is rebound once on return, without automatically selecting another available input. Unknown replacement IDs can be chosen in the existing input selector. Old owned ports close once without waiting/retry; rejected closes are contained. Initialization detaches old callbacks and epochs prevent older permission completions from replacing newer sessions. An existing port selected again during reinitialization is not closed after reopening. Unsupported/failed requests clear stale input UI state.

onStateChange now supplies a reason. Message activity no longer triggers an extra trainer match refresh/full score redraw before its actual grading callback. frame-task.js coalesces only device/debug DOM work; input, capture and grading are synchronous. Bootstrap caches channel/device dropdown options and suppresses intermediate device-transition paints while still publishing state. WaitController publishes either the partial result or the next completed gate, rather than both; bootstrap avoids repainting that result a second time. Non-publishing bend/release paths still refresh trainer/fretboard feedback. Captures continue receiving synchronous score projections; no grading/timing inference was changed.

Report text is revision-cached after evidence changes, and JSON serialization reads the internal plain-data tree without first cloning the entire recording. Public midiCaptureReport objects retain copy isolation. Start/stop, new rows, releases, outcomes and snapshots invalidate correctly. At the capture limit, automatic text generation waits for the last row's outcome instead of serializing twice around that MIDI callback. This reduces avoidable allocation/large-text work; it does not establish that report generation caused the observed USB hang.

## Fix stale bend offsets on the first Am7 — 2026-10-16

midi-pitch.js now distinguishes cached channel bend diagnostics from applicable onset corrections. noteOn records an active raw base/time. Only a bend observed within 60 ms of that active onset anchors an applied correction. A repeated identical raw base can inherit that observed correction, preserving the prior C#4-plus-bend-to-D4 behavior. Changing raw base removes the anchor. Releases end active onset eligibility without by themselves discarding a valid exact-base repeat anchor. A late or unassociated bend revokes it. A new WaitSession resets pitch state, including new attempts, starts, loops and reconfiguration; pause clears it too. This supersedes the previous entry's cross-session/pause bend-state persistence. A bare cached bend never transposes a new onset. No score expectation is used to choose the offset.

This intentionally bounds pitch interpretation to observed attack corrections for the current Fishman workflow. It is not complete expressive MIDI bend support; articulation remains deferred. Pure interpreter output/capture pitch metadata distinguish applied bendValue from observed channelBendValue. Wait fretboard projection uses the same anchored interpreter. Saved ±12 range, raw router messages, native alphaTab behavior, short-note acceptance, genuine wrong-pitch grading, Poly and Realtime are unchanged. No extreme-fret filter was added.

## Wait Hex Note-On plus pitch-bend correction — 2026-10-16

The latest 2,684-message report reaches 97% with nine held-note supports. Owner disputes E7 red fret 8 and asks about measure-7 fret 26. Measure-2 E7 receives D#4 (63) plus -682 pitch bend at the same timestamp; with ±12 sensitivity this is approximately D4 (62), the required fret 7. Bm7b5 in measures 2/4 similarly receives C#4 (61) then +683 after 28/46 ms, producing D4. Measure-4 E7 receives D#4 with centered bend and no compensating update, so the capture does not establish a corrected D4 there. Measure-7 channel 4 E5 has velocity 10 / 55 ms and later G3, consistent with a tracking spike but not proof of a physical fret. No blanket extreme-fret filter exists. Dispatch delay median/95th/max was 4.5/13/27.4 ms, handler 95th/max 5.7/11.4 ms; these do not establish upstream message loss.

Owner confirms ±12 semitones, without a confirmed bend mode. Fishman's official Host guide documents matching pitch-bend ranges and ±12 presets: https://www.fishman.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/07/TriplePlay-Host-User-Guide.pdf (pitch range pp. 35–36 and hardware presets p. 55). The app previously graded only the raw Note-On pitch. New midi-pitch.js separately owns per-channel bend interpretation: raw MIDI + signed bend * range / 8192, rounded to a semitone for existing pitch grading. WaitSession consumes it; raw router normalization/capture remains unchanged. This is scoped to Wait Hex, not broader articulation grading or Poly/Realtime.

Wait initially grades immediately. If a bend arrives within 60 ms of an initially wrong, still-held onset, its interpretation may be reconciled on the original target. A corrected required pitch removes that onset's wrong count, live error and retained wrong-pitch entry, then credits only an unfilled required slot. Other corrected pitches retain one penalty and update their pitch detail. Repeated updates do not add attacks. A historically completed target can be cleaned up without advancing the current target. Released attacks, paused eligibility and updates beyond 60 ms cannot be reused. Already-accepted attacks are not regraded as later expressive bends; bends create no new attacks. This narrow initial stabilization is not a generic short-note rejection or guessed string/fret correction.

The controller shares pitch state across normal session restarts/loops and pauses; reconfiguration recreates it. Raw Note-Off identities release their interpreted held/live-error keys. MIDI settings expose saved Wait pitch-bend range: ±12 on first use per owner confirmation, ±2, ±24 or Ignore bends. Setting changes clear Wait results. The fretboard's Wait Hex held-note projection uses the same pure pitch interpreter; notation/tab continue consuming session results. Capture keeps original raw bytes and initial decisions, adds per-onset pitch metadata and pitch-corrected/pitch-updated outcomes, and snapshots retain correction histories. It never rewrites the recorded input or pretends a new physical attack occurred.

Validation: 269 Node tests pass. Cases cover both observed correction signs, independent channels/ranges, same-timestamp and delayed reconciliation, persistent bends, raw releases, no double advancement, completed-history correction, pause/resume, late/unbent/released/disabled-range errors, still-wrong pitch updates, short notes, saved-range validation and preserved capture evidence. Isolated replays of the supplied measure-2 E7 and measure-2/4 Bm7b5 sequences finish cleanly; uncorrected measure-4 E7 retains two received wrong pitches. No whole-performance accuracy claim from replaying input against altered target advancement. Syntax/whitespace checks pass; actual browser/Fishman acceptance remains pending. No native alphaTab/Live Server/transport, extreme-spike filtering, Piano, version or Git publishing changes.

## Raw-input audit and conservative repeated Hex chords — 2026-10-16

Owner reports striking every chord string despite repeated stalls. Latest 486-message report exactly reproduces 72 correct, five wrong, 94%. The raw capture contains no fresh E4 on several second strums; earlier E4 remains reported held across them. The final C4 was released and followed only by pitch bend, not a fresh C4 attack. This is evidence at the browser listener capture boundary, not proof of a skipped physical string or a fault in the Fishman device. Replaying all recorded raw bytes through the actual router preserves 486/486 messages, with 243 selected-channel deliveries and zero ignored-held-duplicate decisions. No reproducible app-side loss was found; live upstream delivery cannot be established from this replay.

Owner authorized revising chord recognition. New wait-chord-gesture.js is a pure eligibility rule for Wait Collect / One at a time only. On a consecutive identical six-string Hex chord of at least three distinct slots, all but one tone must have fresh accepted attacks on distinct channels in the current bounded gesture. The final tone can be supported by its still-held, actually attacked slot from the previous completed chord, on a different channel. Its original onset must still be current. No support from an inferred prior tone, released/replaced/bent pitch, new chord, X target, Poly, Together, skipped target, multi-missing chord or already-completed target. This remains pitch grading; it adds no exact-position requirement.

Settlement waits min(200 ms, chord window) from gesture start and 60 ms quiet after the last considered note. Existing 50 ms Wait updates settle it within 60 ms of that boundary; old gestures cannot settle after a delayed timer. A genuine wrong note blocks support for that gesture, and a real fourth tone still completes immediately. Session held evidence clears on pause/restart/new attempt and changes with release or noncentral bend. Completed supported notes add chord credit but no invented MIDI message: lastFeedback uses supported-held-tone, history carries freshAttack:false and supportedHeldNotes, results count supportedHeld, diagnostics say reattack unverified, and capture snapshots retain the support list. Help discloses the rule. It does not verify a physical reattack and cannot solve a released note with no fresh pitch evidence.

Capture additionally records receivedTimeMs, dispatchDelayMs (listener-entry time versus MIDI timestamp) and handlerDurationMs (through grading/feedback capture entry). These can expose browser/app backlog in a fresh report; they do not measure pickup latency or prove message loss. Raw routing, normalization and held-duplicate suppression are unchanged.

Validation: 255 Node tests pass, with recording-on/off raw routing, release/velocity-zero/filter/duplicate/bend preservation, delayed controller progression, independent evidence labels, non-chaining, late wrong fret, unchanged full-chord immediacy and mode/layout guards. Changed application modules pass syntax checks and whitespace checks. Isolated target-pair replays resolve all three held-E stalls; the final released-C stall remains pending as intended. No new overall percentage is inferred from replaying the old performance against altered advancement. Actual Live Server/Fishman acceptance remains pending; owner should repeat normal strums, record diagnostics and check a deliberate upper-string error. If the released-C case persists, a separate Fishman-side monitor comparison is needed to locate the missing fresh input. No native alphaTab/Realtime/deferred-feature/Piano changes, version bump or publishing.

## Wait correction penalties and changing-pitch X tails — 2026-10-16

Owner authorized this grading correction after a read-only diagnosis. The latest 1,636-message capture exactly reproduced 260 correct / 58 wrong (82%) through the prior engine. Its mutually exclusive penalty groups were 32 omitted-string inputs, 19 repeats on required strings and seven other wrong-pitch inputs on required strings. Hex noise tolerance was off; enabling it in the prior-engine replay gave 91%. The owner's measure-4 error was received F#4 on string 2 (fret 7 versus expected F4/fret 6). Correct B/A/D reattacks during correction also incurred penalties.

Wait now returns ignored-collected-tone without adding credit, wrong count or wrong-pitch history when a required pitch is already collected. Same-channel Hex repeats cannot fill another unison slot; a distinct channel remains necessary. Together expiry runs first, so a fresh attempt still requires the full chord within its window. Genuine wrong pitches and their retained feedback remain intact; Realtime is unchanged.

The previous X tail guard ended when any seen channel/pitch repeated, allowing a brief changing estimate on string 3 to seed the following pitched target. Percussive tails now ignore the whole burst, including repeated/changing identities. A 60 ms quiet gap (shared chordGroupingMs), or reattack of the starting channel in Hex / starting pitch in Poly at least 60 ms after gesture start, ends the guard. Its existing maximum 200 ms / shorter configured window still bounds it. Pitched-tail rules and ordinary single-note targets are unchanged. This is a bounded gesture heuristic, not a duration filter or reliable physical-strum detector.

Validation: 243 Node tests pass, including synthetic versions of the supplied sequences, multiple X targets, reversed strums, fast singles, unisons, Together expiry and retention of the genuine fret-7 error after replayed correct tones. Application syntax and whitespace checks pass. Existing tests that expected duplicate penalties were updated to the authorized behavior. It cannot establish a new overall percentage. Fresh Fishman/browser acceptance remains pending. ## Muted attacks and chord handoff — 2026-10-16

Owner supplied fvjgfr-002.gp5 and a 628-message TriplePlay capture. Read-only replay reproduced all 149 graded attacks and 43 wrong attacks.
