score-playback retains isDead/stringCount. Mixed targets require only pitched notes; an all-X target retains its written identities but shares one unpitched attack slot. Any fresh accepted Note-On satisfies it in Hex/Poly, Wait/Realtime. Muted strums producing no MIDI still cannot advance Wait. No duration/velocity cutoff or hand-reach inference is introduced. The fretboard does not prescribe an X note's stored fret. Diagnostics label muted targets; Hex fret captions describe a MIDI interpretation.

Wait groups the final gesture of a completed chord and ignores its not-yet-seen previous chord tones at the following gate, within at most 200 ms from gesture start (or the shorter configured chord window). Repeated channel identity in Hex / pitch identity in Poly starts a new gesture; singles have no guard. All-X tails use the same guard. This bounded heuristic needs hardware acceptance, especially fast arpeggios and reversed strums. Realtime keeps compensated playback windows and favors remaining tones of a started chord within its existing 60 ms grouping tolerance over a nearby occurrence. Ordinary repeated-note matching remains intact.

Practice exposes saved Allow muted-string noise (Hex), off = Strict. Enabled six-string chord targets ignore omitted-string input without crediting required tones from that input. Required-string errors, unknown mappings, single notes, other string counts and Poly stay strict. Tolerance necessarily forgives intentional extra notes on omitted strings. Setting changes pause/reset results; score loads retain the choice. Native rendering, input normalization, media sync and Live Server workflow are unchanged.

## Persistent chord-diagram placement — 2026-10-13

Display now has a **Chord diagrams** selector in the agreed order: **Score Default**, **Above Chord**, **Top of Score**, **Off**. The selection persists in `gts.displayPreferences`. Score Default restores the current score's original native alphaTab stylesheet values for global top/inline diagrams and per-track top placement; a new score captures its own defaults before the user's saved override is applied. Above Chord uses alphaTab's in-score diagram rendering at chord beats; Top of Score enables the native deduplicated diagram area and clears per-track top restrictions for a consistent global choice; Off hides both diagram renderings without removing chord names. alphaTab remains the rendering/engraving owner. ## Relink sync profiles to recovered local media — 2026-10-13

Selecting a saved profile for the current score and choosing a local recording now explicitly relinks that profile when the file name, size or modified date changed. This preserves existing sync points through drive recovery/renaming; the changed file metadata is saved to the selected profile after its points pass validation against the active score measures/repeats and media duration. Profiles from another score or source type are rejected, and a failed validation surfaces an error without applying invalid points. Automatic profile matching still uses existing local metadata until the user explicitly relinks. No recording bytes are stored and no score, alphaTab or grading behavior changed. ## Ignore duplicate Note-On while a pitch is held — 2026-10-13

The supplied MIDI capture showed the same channel-4 E4 Note-On twice, 27 ms apart, with no Note-Off until about 1.28 seconds after the second message. The owner confirmed they held the note. `midi-router.js` now suppresses a repeated Note-On for an already-held channel/pitch before it reaches either Wait or Realtime grading. The duplicate remains visible in the optional MIDI capture report as `ignored-held-duplicate`; the original onset remains the start of the reported hold duration. A matching Note-Off releases the gate, so a later attack counts normally. This blocks the observed held-note from satisfying the next repeated-pitch target and does not add a wrong-note penalty. Router and capture regressions are automated; the owner still needs to check keyboard and Fishman behavior in Live Server/browser, especially deliberate repeated notes and Hex channel releases.

## Piano-style Loop menu and count-in — 2026-10-11

Started from clean Git status. Replaced the old card/dropdown/Apply Loop form with the compact Piano reference, reusing menu-controls.css control dimensions, native sliders, plain number fields and horizontal down/up arrows. Arrows step by one; crossing start/end pushes the other endpoint. Updates apply immediately and keep the previous pause/reset boundary. Both loop switches default off on page/score load and remain session-only; disabled Loop Count-in retains its choice for re-enabling during that score. Measure values follow generated playback order rather than conflating repeat occurrences; repeated scores show the written measure/occurrence labels.

Verified bundled alphaTab provides isLooping, playbackRange, countInVolume, native mouse selection and public rendering bounds, but no default player menu. Realtime continues to use native tick-range playback. Native continuous loops do not rerun their count-in. The app therefore schedules a shared one-bar pre-roll from its already rendered native AlphaSynth/Sonivox click, before starting/resuming either mode. It uses the starting measure's meter and effective tempo (Wait's fixed reference BPM), saved metronome volume even when metronome is off, and a cancellation generation. A saved volume of zero mutes count-in too. Initial/resume Play always counts in; repeat pre-roll is opt-in. Repeat count-in lets the native range finish/rewind, then starts native playback after pre-roll; otherwise isLooping remains enabled. Main score playback and Realtime grading stay paused during pre-roll. Wait adds a count-in phase, ignores attacks until ready, and excludes pre-roll from resumed Together active time. No grading formula or timing calibration changes.

Native beat dragging is handled via beatMouseDown/Move/Up, after alphaTab produces its tick selection. Expand that range to complete playback measures, synchronize the menu, and enable looping. This avoids custom pointer/hit testing. Single clicks restore the app's enabled range instead of silently clearing it; host seek callbacks reject outside ticks and return to loop start. Mouse selection inherits native repeat resolution; the menu remains available for explicitly choosing another occurrence. Gray shading is a separate pointer-transparent SVG generated from native master-bar bounds, refreshed after rendering and range changes. Written measures used by any selected occurrence remain unshaded; an occurrence cannot have its own separate score rectangle. Shading never filters targets or changes grades.

## Metronome timing/restart fix and saved volume — 2026-10-11

The supplied Wes Montgomery - Four On Six.gp3 was parsed with the bundled alphaTab importer: its initial tempo is 200 BPM, with two 200 BPM automations in bar 1 and no other tempo changes. The previous 120 BPM display was a fallback; this check does not establish that earlier native playback was at 120.

Owner reported visual pulse preceding audible click, erratic Wait pulse, and Wait audio becoming silent after option/pause/restart use. Native playback events report samples as an output block is processed, before the speaker output timestamp. Realtime pulses now account for the event's position within the processed block and map to the browser audio output timestamp, with reported output/base latency as fallback. Queued pulses cancel on pause, seek, disable and mode exit. This follows the browser timestamp contract: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/AudioContext/getOutputTimestamp.

Wait no longer loops a live secondary ScriptProcessor synth. The host renders one click using bundled native AlphaSynth/Sonivox, then schedules copies on the main player's Web Audio clock. Audio sources and visual pulses share each scheduled beat; stop cancels both. Restart uses fresh sources and no old circular-buffer counters. UI refreshes, volume changes and cursor/loop movement do not restart the clock; BPM changes do. Stalled scheduling skips missed clicks instead of a burst. Latest desired state still governs completion of asynchronous font loading. No changes to native score playback, grading, input routing, output selection or deferred features.

Default volume is now 75%. Valid volume changes (including 0) save as gts.metronomeVolumePercent. Startup restores a valid saved 0–100 value before displaying controls; missing/invalid/inaccessible storage falls back to 75. Metronome enable remains off at startup, visual pulse remains session-only. Storage belongs to the browser origin: localhost and 127.0.0.1 can retain different preferences.

Realtime grading review (read-only): the evaluator anchors to native playback notifications at their receipt time and interpolates MIDI timestamps. Manual MIDI latency compensation exists, but speaker output latency is not automatically applied to grading. A note played to delayed audible backing can appear late. Record this for a separately scoped browser/Fishman timing/calibration check; do not change grading as part of this metronome task.

Automated evidence: 152 tests pass, including real bundled click samples, late font-load cancellation, six pause/restart cycles, fixed 200 BPM scheduling, stalled-tab skip, muted visual beats, pulse/audio cancellation, output timestamp mapping and storage validation/failure. Changed modules pass Node syntax checks. Background browser smoke check loaded the actual GP3, verified 200 BPM/75% initial volume, Realtime and Wait pulses, Wait pause/resume, and 65% surviving reload with metronome off. Test browser did not have MIDI permission; physical Fishman and speaker listening remain owner acceptance. Check audible/visual alignment in both modes, repeated option/mode toggles and restarts, and volume retention using your usual Live Server address. No Git publishing or version changes.


## Tempo menu and independent Wait metronome — 2026-10-11

Implemented the owner's Piano-style reference with compact BPM/speed rows, linked sliders/number boxes, reset, and stacked arrows snapping to the next/previous multiple of five. Wait latches the source BPM upon mode entry (or score readiness), then scales that reference by the shared speed percentage; advancing/seeking/looping the score does not alter its rate. Wait BPM/speed edits keep the attempt running and preserve results/partial chords. Realtime keeps native tempo changes and the previous pause/reset path. Grading formulas and Wait stepping remain unchanged.

The host creates a second native AlphaSynth with a looping rest-only score and the same bundled Sonivox font for Wait; it never seeks/plays the practice score. Native ScriptProcessor output needs at least two 4096-frame buffers to request samples; a browser check exposed silence with a 100ms buffer. Corrected to 400ms and strengthened the regression to exercise native output callbacks and nonzero click samples, rather than a simulated sample-request pump alone. The secondary synth pauses on manual Pause, Reset, completion, mode exit or score replacement; solved notes/loop passes do not restart it. Its pulse uses played-sample events. The main native renderer, transport and assets are preserved.

## 2026-10-10 — Notation feedback: played-pitch noteheads and explicit accidentals

User approved a notation-first visual increment based on Piano Trainer screenshots. Replace the on-score wrong-pitch text with separate red noteheads at the played pitch's actual staff line/space, horizontally anchored to the target. Keep live held notes disappearing on release and retained mistakes reappearing on target completion. Color expected standard-notation noteheads with filled green/red overlays instead of rectangular outlines; keep blue current-target guidance and original tab outlines. Completed Wait targets with any received wrong pitch remain red even when finally solved correctly.

The user delegated accidental presentation. Decision: explicit accidentals, including naturals, on these supplemental feedback notes, even when present in the key signature. This avoids reliance on the key/earlier score accidentals without changing native notation or accidental state. Spelling prefers tones supplied by the local bar's key signature, then its sharp/flat direction (Bb in Bb major, A# in a sharp key). MIDI cannot uniquely determine enharmonic intent. No Poly string/fret inference or new Hex tab markers were added.

`notation-feedback.js` owns pure spelling/projection and native-bounds overlay geometry. The host passes the current engraving metrics/zoom and renders SVG ellipses, vector accidentals and ledger lines without modifying the score. Staff projection follows the bundled native five-line treble/bass/alto/tenor conventions, beat/clef ottava and display transposition. It is verified against already-rendered original noteheads; unsupported or inconsistent geometry omits wrong dots instead of guessing. ## 2026-10-10 — Realtime Pause protection without predicted beats

User reported Realtime still clears on Pause after the first follow-up. That fix relied on guessing the API's current cursor beat from the host's last received tick. Those can differ because worker position delivery and native cursor rendering are asynchronous. Added regression coverage invokes the actual bundled native Pause handler with a cursor beat different from the received position; it fails against the previous host. A second regression reproduces a queued non-seek backward position update clearing results while already paused; it fails against the previous engine.

Manual Pause now immediately freezes app-side grading and marks one pending native Pause seek, without guessing its destination. The acknowledgement reanchors without clearing results. Play, stopped events and deliberate score mouse-down clear unused protection, so a later user seek is not swallowed. Non-seek backward updates while paused reanchor without starting a new attempt; explicit seeks and backward playback while running retain reset semantics. Native alphaTab cursor/audio behavior is unchanged. New attempt here remains available and Play/Resume remains retention-preserving.

99 tests pass, with both new regression scenarios demonstrated failing against the previous implementation and passing now. Tests also cover native synchronous and worker-style Pause delivery, intentional score clicks after a pause without a snap, Reset, completion/replay and all existing Wait/new-attempt behavior. No browser/physical-MIDI validation has been performed here; this correction still needs user acceptance with the original failing setup. Deliver only the eight files changed since New attempt here, including the updated project notes.

## 2026-10-10 — New attempt at current position

User agreed to preserve Play/Resume retention and add an explicit **New attempt here** action in Practice. It clears grading, outlines, and Wait live/retained/pass history without moving the cursor or changing running/paused state. Wait retains the active gate (including an outstanding cursor acknowledgement), clears partial chord hits/retries and resets its Together clock; prior targets are skipped for this new attempt. Realtime rebuilds results at the current tick and reanchors its clock, so it can continue immediately when already playing. Existing reset/seek/loop behavior and score formulas remain unchanged. At a completed/end position the action clears the summary in place; Play still follows the existing completed replay behavior.

`attempt-controls.js` owns the button wiring/readiness only; bootstrap routes the intent and clears stale held MIDI display; the engine/controller own the session reset. The action is disabled until the native player and practice targets are ready. 98 tests pass, including 4 new tests for paused partial Together chords, clearing previous loop-pass review, in-flight gate preservation, Realtime running/paused continuation, and button readiness. Local browser/MIDI acceptance is still required. Deliver only this update's changed/new files and a git commit description.

## 2026-10-10 — Realtime Pause beat-snap correction

User confirmed Wait retains its percentage but reported Realtime still clears on Pause. The bundled full AlphaTabApi `_onPlayerStateChanged` seeks to the current beat start for a non-stopped Paused event. This can move backward from mid-beat and was absent from the earlier synth-only test. The host now identifies the expected one-shot Pause seek using the rendered tracks and native tick lookup, marks it `isPauseSnap`, and keeps input paused. The engine reanchors the clock/cursor without clearing results, percentage, or note states. Native beat snapping is preserved; deliberate seeks, Reset, loop rewinds and completed replay retain their reset rules. No UI or scoring formula changes.

94 tests pass. The host regression invokes the bundled API's actual Pause handler mid-beat with a mixed 50% score and covers both synchronous seek-before-state and worker-style state-before-seek delivery. Engine regression verifies retained mixed results, ignored paused attacks, Resume and subsequent explicit seek reset. Browser/physical MIDI acceptance remains pending: pause mid-beat several times in Realtime, including within a loop, resume, and confirm Reset clears the score. Deliver only files changed since the Stage 8 ZIP, with a git commit description.

## 2026-10-10 — Stage 8 retention and first Wait feedback increments

Inspected the bundled native synthesizer: ordinary Pause changes player state without seeking; no failing ordinary Pause sequence was reproduced by the baseline tests. The prior engine nevertheless reset on every `isSeek` notification. Stage 8 preserves unchanged-position seek acknowledgements through Pause/Resume, while actual seeks/loop rewinds still reset. No claim is made that all hardware causes of the user's original report have been reproduced.

Wait now stores received wrong pitches by event occurrence, retains them through completion/expiry/Pause, and uses one completed-with-errors outcome for every note in an error chord. Original score/tab content is untouched. Live errors release independently by channel/pitch, including while paused, and clear on completion. Current/latest occurrences own written-score overlays; previous loop passes retain review history while new pass scoring stays fresh.

First notation increment uses red actual pitch-name/MIDI text at native standard-notation target bounds, live while held and retained after completion. Display can hide live labels or history independently. Practice lists completed mistakes by pass/measure/occurrence/target. Precise staff notehead glyphs and verified-Hex temporary fret labels remain explicit next increments: do not guess glyph y from unlaid-out accidental-helper values or equate configured channels/MPE with verified physical strings. The latest TODO specification is still the final intended behavior.

Percentage formulas are unchanged. Duplicate attacks of an already-collected required pitch retain the previous wrong-attack penalty, but are not received-wrong pitches and do not enter pitch history or turn solved targets red. Together timeouts remain retries without wrong/missed penalties. This distinction is documented rather than silently changing scoring.

93 tests pass, including native synth transport integration and native SVG bounds across supported profiles/zoom, host redraw/visibility, independent releases, chords, retries, resets and occurrence/pass isolation. Browser alignment and physical MIDI remain pending; see STAGE-8.md for local checks and limits. Deferred features and Piano Trainer are untouched.

## 2026-10-08 — Stage 7 Wait chord options

The deferred Together / Collect notes option is implemented for Wait. Collect remains the default for continuity. Together starts a real-millisecond window on the first correct chord attack, default 200 ms and configurable 50–1000 ms. Expiry clears provisional hits and increments one retry, without wrong/missed penalties. A late pitch may start the next attempt. Wrong pitches retain their normal penalty and do not start/extend the window.

Pause excludes elapsed paused time and ignores input, preserving an in-window partial chord. Single-note targets remain immediate. Loop/session resets preserve selected settings but clear attempt state. Realtime retains its prior 60 ms chord grouping and timing windows. Preferences remain page-session only.

79 automated tests pass, including strict window boundaries, idle expiry, retry scoring, unisons, multiple channels, pause, loop/settings reset, and form validation. Keyboard/guitar browser testing remains pending.

## Long-score clipped audio — 2026-11-13

Read the three supplied Arirang GPX/GP/XML exports without copying them into fixtures or library storage. Reversing the registered patches in an ignored investigation folder reconstructed pristine alphaTab 1.8.4 with both recorded upstream SHA-256 hashes. Pristine and patched classic readers generated identical normalized note commands and remaining MIDI events for each supplied file (2,924 GPX/GP notes; 2,957 XML notes). The issue reproduced in isolated installed Chrome as audio-buffer starvation. A five-second CPU profile identified repeated full-score record searches in Realtime feedback history and future-target held-note checks, rather than altered vendor note timing. The initial GPX replay advanced only 1.877 seconds in five wall seconds, with 1,186 empty worklet blocks. Indexed record lookup and bounded settlement removed that bottleneck; browser-frame visual coalescing provides further room for native audio delivery while retaining every grading update. Private evidence stays in ignored dist/arirang-investigation. The original 160-bar regression and all three supplied exports cover opening and late-score playback; audible/browser/Fishman acceptance remains an owner check.

Validation: all 563 Node tests and changed-module syntax checks pass. The new original long-score runner fails against the pre-fix Realtime module (1.109 seconds advanced in 4.060 wall seconds; 1,109 empty versus 384 filled blocks). With the final fix, both synthetic checkpoints and all six supplied-file checkpoints have zero steady-playback empty blocks and essentially real-time clock progress. No version bump, vendor replacement or private Lesson Bridge edit. Existing pending vendor work is preserved separately.

Final isolated Chrome checks also pass Realtime written windows/late chord feedback in Poly and Hex, sync-practice/editor transitions and recording seeks, full-recording intro/outro/loop/Pause/Resume, and fast-Pause cancellation. These use synthetic MIDI/media and do not establish audible or physical Fishman acceptance.
